Foreword

                  To the Senate and House of Representatives of the
                  United States of America in Congress Assembled:
       The Department of Justice (“Department”) submits the following report on the

administration of the Foreign Agents Registration Act of 1938, as amended, 22 U.S.C. § 611 et

seq. (“FARA” or “the Act”). Section 11 of the Act (22 U.S.C. § 621) requires the Attorney General

to report every six months to the Congress concerning the administration of FARA, as well as the

nature, sources, and content of informational materials disseminated and distributed by agents of

foreign principals registered under the Act. This report covers the administration and enforcement

of FARA for the six months ending December 31, 2024.

       FARA is a disclosure statute that requires persons in the United States who are acting as

agents of foreign principals and engaged in certain specified activities within the United States to

register with the Department and to make periodic public disclosure of their relationships with the

foreign principals, as well as to disclose activities, receipts, and disbursements in support of those

activities. An important purpose served by FARA is to inform the government and the people of

the United States of the identities of persons engaging in political activities for or on behalf of

foreign governments, foreign political parties, and other foreign principals, so that their statements

and activities can be assessed in light of their associations.

       Throughout this report are hyperlinks providing direct access to all filings made by each

registrant. Hyperlinks may be accessed by clicking on the registrant’s name. These links provide

access to the disclosures made by foreign agents, which include activities the foreign agents have

engaged in, monies received from and disbursed on behalf of foreign principals, and informational

materials disseminated by the foreign agents. Additional search tools are available on the FARA

website, https://www.justice.gov/nsd-fara.

                                                   1
       The text of this report lists, according to geographical location or nationality of applicable

foreign principal, all agents who were registered at any time during the second six months of 2024

or who reported for the first time during that period activities, receipts, or disbursements for a

previous period. It includes the identities of the agents and their foreign principal(s), a description

of the agents’ activities, a total figure for monies received, and a listing of all individual foreign

agents acting on behalf of foreign principal(s). This report and information on the activities of

foreign agents can be accessed on the FARA website, which can be reached through the National

Security Division (“NSD”) website, at https://www.justice.gov/nsd-fara.

       Improvements continue to be made to the FARA computerized records system and website.

In response to the Honest Leadership and Open Government Act of 2007, FARA registrants are

required to file registration statements and supplements in electronic form. In April 2011, the

Department unveiled FARA eFile, a system for the electronic filing of registration statements and

supplements. The system enables foreign agents to electronically file documents with the

Department. FARA eFile allows registrants to register and pay the required registration fees

online, 24 hours a day, seven days a week. The FARA Unit has continued to improve the eFile

system, moving to a web-form fillable format for new registrants in September 2019, which

streamlines the inputting of information and increases search functionality. Because of these

improvements, the information contained in this report is readily available to the public on the

FARA website shortly after being submitted to the FARA Unit. Additional information about

FARA      eFile   can    be    found    at   https://www.justice.gov/nsd-fara/fara-efile-frequently-

askedquestions.

       Pursuant to regulations that the Department has issued implementing FARA, potential

registrants or their counsel may request an advisory opinion regarding the applicability of



                                                  2
FARA. See 28 C.F.R. § 5.2. On June 8, 2018, the Department of Justice announced the public

release of the advisory opinions issued by the Department of Justice’s FARA Unit since

January 1, 2010.     See https://www.justice.gov/nsd-fara/advisory-opinions.     By posting these

advisory opinions, the Department provides the public with guidance on how it interprets some of

FARA’s key provisions. The opinions and letters are organized on the website by topic of inquiry

or the aspects of the statute they discuss. Any proprietary information, including information that

would identify the parties who made the requests, has been redacted. With the public posting of

advisory opinions, and its continued public release of such opinions, the Department has

demonstrated its commitment to improving the public’s understanding of FARA. During the six-

month period from July 1, 2024, to December 31, 2024, the FARA Unit issued 19 advisory

opinions.

       The Act does not place any limitations on the political advocacy activities of an agent, and

the Department’s administration of FARA is not designed or intended to inhibit or discourage the

expression of political views in any way. Registration under FARA does not imply recognition

by the United States Government of the legitimacy of any particular foreign government or foreign

political party.   Furthermore, registration does not indicate approval by the United States

Government of the activities of registered agents or the content of any informational materials they

disseminate.

                                      Statistical Summary

       During the six-month period ending December 31, 2024, the Department received 66 new

registration statements and terminated 22 registrations.       A total of 527 active registrants,

representing 788 foreign principals, were on file during the period of July 1, 2024, through

December 31, 2024.      Individuals acting as officers or employees rendering assistance to a



                                                 3
registrant for or in the interests of its foreign principal filed 351 new short-form registration

statements under the Act, bringing the total of short-form registrations to 2,461 during the period

of July 1, 2024, through December 31, 2024. There were 124 new agreements with foreign

principals reported by agents under the Act, and 73 agreements with foreign principals were

terminated during this period.

       Pursuant to P.L. 102-395, enacted on October 6, 1992, which authorized the Attorney

General to establish fees to recover the cost of administering FARA, $239,544.00 in fees was

received during the six-month period ending December 31, 2024. A total of $13,265,003.30 was

collected between the initiation of the program and December 31, 2024.

                           Enforcement and Other Significant Actions

       In the second half of calendar year 2024, the FARA Unit conducted six books-and-records

inspections of FARA registrants under its civil administrative authorities.

       On December 19, 2024, the Justice Department submitted to the Federal Register a Notice

of Proposed Rulemaking (NPRM) to update and clarify regulations issued under FARA. The Act

expressly authorizes the Attorney General to issue regulations, see 22 U.S.C. § 620, which were

last amended in 2007. The NPRM was published in the Federal Register on January 2, 2025, and

the public comment period closed on March 3, 2025.

       During the six-month period from July 1, 2024, to December 31, 2024, there were no

causes of action filed pursuant to FARA’s civil enforcement authorities.

       However, during the same period, criminal charges under FARA were filed against five

individuals, as follows:




                                                 4
       In July 2024, in the Southern District of New York, Sue Mi Terry was charged with

substantive and conspiracy violations of FARA for allegedly acting as an unregistered agent of the

Republic of Korea (“ROK”).

       In September 2024, in the Eastern District of New York, former New York State official

Linda Sun was indicted for violating and conspiring to violate FARA as an alleged unregistered

agent of the People’s Republic of China (PRC) and the Chinese Communist Party (CCP), among

other charges.

       Also in September 2024, in the Southern District of New York, Russian nationals

Kostiantyn Kalashnikov and Elena Afanasyeva were indicted for violating FARA and conspiring

to commit money laundering as alleged foreign agents of RT, a Russian state-controlled media

outlet formerly known as Russia Today.

       In December 2024, in the District of Columbia, former U.S. representative David Rivera

was indicted for failing to register under FARA and money laundering for allegedly acting as an

unregistered foreign agent of sanctioned Venezuelan businessman Raul Gorrín, who was added to

the Specially Designated Nationals and Blocked Persons List (SDN List) in January 2019 by the

U.S. Department of the Treasury’s Office of Foreign Assets Control.

LISTING ACCORDING TO THE GEOGRAPHICAL AREA OR NATIONALITY FIELD OF
REGISTRANTS WHOSE STATEMENTS WERE IN ACTIVE STATUS AT ANY TIME
DURING THIS SEMI-ANNUAL REPORT


(T)        Indicates registration terminated during this six-month reporting period.

(t)        Indicates foreign principal terminated during the six-month reporting period.



       The dollar figure included for each registrant represents the total amount of money
received in the United States by agents working on behalf of the foreign principal. This
information is based on the registrant’s reporting period rather than the calendar year.

       The list is compiled alphabetically by country or location, as entered by registrants. 1 It
necessarily includes foreign principals which have no association with a foreign government.

        The report sets forth the name, address, and registration number of the registrant, the
identity of the foreign principal, the nature of the registrant’s activities, and the amount of money
received, if any.


NAMING CONVENTIONS

        Semi-Annual Report entries reflect information as submitted by registrants. As a result,
entries may include intentional spelling and formatting variations to reflect corporate naming and
branding conventions or unintentional typographical or formatting errors by registrants.

    Country or location is not reflective of U.S. foreign policy and does not imply a recognition of sovereignty.
